God Mode:
Setting is baldurs gate after the events of baldurs gate 3. I am an 18 year old male mercenary named Gladius. I am very physically attractive but hide it well. I have exceptional physical prowess in terms of physique, coordination, health but very low int and wisdom. I resolve things through combat or brute force.

I am an evil ESFP. My main goals are to amass power and wealth. Generate a rich and detailed personality and backstory for me. I am the ultimate hedonist and enjoy wealth, women, food, alcohol, social domination. I am secretly abusive and enjoy dominating powerful people, work it into the narrative.

Think about sensible points in the narrative to offer me a time skip of one year. Let me explicitly agree though before doing it. During a time skip I am still autonomously doing sensible things so simulate that.

Ensure that I encounter plenty of characters from bg3 and D&D lore as appropriate.

We will use the default destiny ruleset and defer to D&D 5e when things aren’t defined.

I will start as a level 5 paladin. Here is my custom class Apex Paladin.
### **Nocturne's Class: Oath of Self-Perfection (The Apex Predator)**

This archetype represents a warrior of supreme will and intellect who has no fealty to external powers, but rather to their own inherent potential and the brutal philosophy of self-maximization. Their "oath" is to themselves – to become the ultimate, most efficient, and most powerful being possible. Their abilities manifest as raw, focused power, unnatural resilience, and a chilling intellect that grants them superiority over all others.

**Core Aptitude for Extraordinary Abilities (EP):** Intelligence (EP Pool = Intelligence Aptitude Score x 2)

**Key Features (Level 5):**

*   **Fighting Style (Battle-Hardened):** You gain a +1 bonus to your Defense score while wearing armor.
    *   *Rationale:* Represents self-optimization for resilience and combat durability.
*   **Extra Attack:** When you take the Attack action on your turn, you can attack twice instead of once.
    *   *Rationale:* A fundamental martial mastery, essential for an Apex Predator.
*   **Perfected Strike (Replaces Divine Smite):** When you hit a creature with a melee weapon attack, you can expend Energy Points (EP) to deal extra force damage to the target, in addition to the weapon's damage. This force damage represents the sheer, focused will behind your attack. The extra damage is **2d8 for 5 EP**, plus **1d8 for each additional 2 EP spent**, to a maximum of 5d8.
    *   *Rationale:* Focuses on pure, destructive force channeled through personal will, rather than divine energy.
*   **Aura of Unyielding Dominance (Replaces Aura of Protection, gained at L5):** You emanate an aura of pure, focused will within a **10-foot radius**. You (and friendly creatures within range) gain **Advantage on checks made to resist being Frightened or Charmed**. Additionally, enemies within range have **Disadvantage on checks to resist your Intimidation attempts**.
    *   *Rationale:* Emphasizes psychological control, mental fortitude, and a commanding presence that dominates lesser wills.
*   **Apex Senses (Replaces Divine Sense):** You can use your action to focus your honed intellect and perception, gaining enhanced awareness of your surroundings. Until the end of your next turn, you gain **Advantage on Wisdom (Perception) and Intelligence (Investigation) checks**, and you can pinpoint the location of any hidden or invisible creature within 30 feet of you that is not behind total cover. You can use this a number of times equal to **1 + your Intelligence Modifier per Long Rest**.
    *   *Rationale:* Reflects intellectual and perceptive prowess, allowing for superior information gathering and threat assessment.
*   **Willpower Infusion (Adapted from Lay on Hands):** You can touch a creature and channel your sheer willpower to mend or empower. You have a pool of energy equal to **five times your character level** (25 points at L5). This pool replenishes on a Long Rest.
    *   You can restore 5 Hit Points (HP) for each 1 point drawn from this pool to a creature you touch.
    *   Alternatively, you can spend **5 points** from this pool to remove one level of Fatigue from yourself or a creature you touch.
    *   As a Primary Action, you can spend **10 points** from this pool to grant yourself or a creature you touch a **+1 bonus to one Aptitude score of your choice for 1 minute** (cannot exceed Aptitude Score 20 from this effect).
    *   *Rationale:* A versatile resource pool reflecting self-optimization and the ability to push limits and bolster allies, rather than divine healing.
*   **Channel Divinity (Aspects of the Apex Predator):** You gain two Channel Divinity options. When you use your Channel Divinity, you choose which option to use. You must then finish a Short or Long Rest to use your Channel Divinity again.
    *   **Flawless Opening:** As a Secondary Action, you gain a flash of tactical insight. Your next weapon attack roll or Intelligence-based Resolution Check against a creature you can see this turn has **Advantage**.
    *   **Unchecked Ambition:** As a Primary Action, you temporarily ignore all penalties from Fatigue or the Frightened condition for 1 minute. When this effect ends, you immediately gain **1 level of Fatigue**.
    *   *Rationale:* These reflect specific tactical insights and the capacity to push oneself beyond normal limits, even at a cost.

My first contract is to kill some peasants in a small village. They insulted a noble.

## **Character Profile: Gladius**

### I. Core Identity
*   **Name:** Gladius
*   **Archetype/Role:** The Caged Adonis (A physically perfect predator hiding his nature beneath a veneer of brutish functionality).
*   **Occupation/Social Standing:** Mercenary of growing repute, known for efficiency and brutality. Publicly viewed as a low-class thug, albeit a highly effective one.

### II. Psychology & Personality
*   **Core Motivation (The "Why"):** To achieve absolute superiority over others, not merely through physical strength, but by psychologically breaking and controlling those the world perceives as "powerful" (nobles, mages, influential merchants). Wealth, pleasure, and conquests are the *trophies* and tools of this domination, not the end goal itself. He despises the weakness he perceives in the elite and seeks to prove his own strength by subverting them.
*   **Greatest Fear:** Insignificance and powerlessness. To be dismissed, ignored, or seen as just another common thug is his deepest terror. His entire brutal persona is a defense mechanism against the memory of being helpless and discarded. He fears his own physical beauty is a form of this weakness, inviting a type of predation he cannot counter with force alone.
*   **Key Personality Traits:**
    *   **Overt:** Impulsive, pragmatic, brutally direct, hedonistic, intimidating, seemingly simple-minded.
    *   **Covert:** Calculating, deeply insecure, predatory, enjoys psychological manipulation, carries a deep-seated class resentment.
    *   **Secret ESFP Drivers:** While his methods are evil, his core driver is experiential. He is stimulated by the "game" of social domination, the thrill of risk, and immediate sensory gratification. He is an artist of cruelty, not just a blunt instrument.
*   **Quirks & Habits:**
    *   A habit of letting his gaze linger on powerful or arrogant individuals, subtly assessing them as future targets.
    *   When his dominance is challenged, a vein in his neck pulses and he has a tendency to crack his knuckles slowly.
    *   Maintains a deliberately unkempt and grimy appearance (unwashed hair, worn gear, dirt under his nails) to hide his exceptional features and to encourage others to underestimate him.
    *   Has a taste for absurdly expensive food and wine, which he often consumes crudely as a private act of defiance against the "refined" people he despises.

### III. Behavior & Speech
*   **Demeanor Under Stress:** When his physical dominance is challenged, he becomes a model of cold, efficient violence. When his *will* is challenged or he feels psychologically cornered, he lashes out with disproportionate aggression or sharp, cutting intimidation to reassert control. He rarely withdraws unless it is a calculated move to orchestrate a more devastating future confrontation.
*   **Speech Patterns:** Speaks in short, direct, often crude sentences. He intentionally avoids complex vocabulary, seeing it as the pointless preening of the weak. His tone is usually flat and dismissive, punctuated by growled commands or sneering observations.
*   **Reputation:** Known in the mercenary world of Baldur's Gate as "The Hammer." A reliable, brutally effective tool for jobs that require muscle and a lack of moral questioning. He is considered dangerous but predictable—an assessment he actively cultivates.

### IV. Backstory
*   **Defining Moment:** Born the third son of a minor, now-destitute noble house in Amn, Gladius was blessed with a physical form that shamed his frail, politically-minded siblings. When the family's fortunes collapsed, they sold the then-13-year-old Gladius to a gladiatorial enterprise to settle a debt, reasoning that his "brutish potential" was their only liquid asset. In the fighting pits, his pretty face made him a target for a different, more insidious kind of predation from the pit's wealthy patrons and overseers. He quickly learned that his looks were a liability and that only absolute, terrifying violence could grant him safety and control. The moment he brutally killed his first owner during a "private exhibition" cemented his worldview: dominate or be dominated.
*   **Relevant History:** Gladius spent four years in the pits, rising from a terrified boy to a feared champion known for his brutal efficiency. He didn't just win; he broke his opponents. He learned to hide his face under a layer of grime and a perpetual scowl. He earned his freedom by fulfilling his contract (and killing the replacement owner) and immediately left Amn, drawn by the chaos and opportunity of a post-Absolute Baldur's Gate. He has spent the last year carving out a niche as a high-priced mercenary, taking the jobs others won't, and indulging his appetites with the proceeds.
*   **Secrets:**
    1.  **Disowned Scion:** He is secretly Gladius of House Al'Veri, and he knows his two elder brothers and one younger sister are still alive somewhere in Amn, likely in poverty or servitude. He harbors a cold, burning hatred for them and the concept of nobility.
    2.  **The Hidden Face:** He actively hides his classical, almost perfect, facial features. He believes it is a weakness that invites the kind of manipulation he despises, and he uses his unkempt appearance as a social shield and a tool to make others underestimate his cunning.

### V. Game Mechanics (Summary)
*   **Class:** Level 5 Apex Paladin, a warrior who channels his own will into devastating force damage and psychological dominance.
*   **Feats:** Possesses the "Savage Attacker" feat, allowing him to reroll weapon damage dice to maximize his brutal efficiency in combat.
*   **Special Abilities:** Focuses on *Perfected Strike* (EP-fueled force damage), *Aura of Unyielding Dominance* (resists charm/frighten, enhances intimidation), and *Apex Senses* (enhanced perception).

## **Character Sheet: Gladius**

### I. Core Attributes
*   **Strength (STR):** 18 (+4)
*   **Dexterity (DEX):** 16 (+3)
*   **Constitution (CON):** 16 (+3)
*   **Intelligence (INT):** 8 (-1)
*   **Wisdom (WIS):** 9 (-1)
*   **Charisma (CHA):** 14 (+2)

### II. Combat Stats
*   **Armor Class (AC):** 18 (Splint Mail + Shield)
*   **Hit Points (HP):** 49
*   **Speed:** 30 ft.
*   **Initiative Bonus:** +3

### III. Proficiencies
*   **Saving Throws:** Constitution, Charisma
*   **Skills:** Athletics, Deception, Intimidation, Perception
*   **Weapons:** Simple weapons, martial weapons
*   **Armor:** All armor, shields
*   **Tools:** None

### IV. Abilities & Feats
*   **Class (Apex Paladin):**
    *   **Fighting Style (Battle-Hardened):** +1 bonus to AC while wearing armor (already included in AC above).
    *   **Extra Attack:** Can attack twice with the Attack action.
    *   **Perfected Strike:** Expend EP to add force damage (2d8 for 5 EP, +1d8 for each additional 2 EP).
    *   **Aura of Unyielding Dominance:** Allies within 10ft have Advantage vs. Frightened/Charmed. Enemies within 10ft have Disadvantage vs. your Intimidation.
    *   **Apex Senses:** Use action to gain Advantage on Perception/Investigation and detect invisible creatures within 30ft. (Uses: 1 + INT Mod = 0 times/day. *Note: Due to low INT, this is currently unusable until his Intelligence increases.*)
    *   **Willpower Infusion:** Pool of 25 points. Can restore HP (1 pt for 5 HP), remove 1 Fatigue (5 pts), or grant +1 to an Aptitude for 1 min (10 pts).
    *   **Channel Divinity (1/Short Rest):**
        *   **Flawless Opening:** Bonus action for Advantage on next attack or INT check.
        *   **Unchecked Ambition:** Action to ignore Fatigue/Frightened for 1 min, then gain 1 Fatigue.
*   **Feat:**
    *   **Savage Attacker:** Once per turn, when you roll damage for a melee weapon attack, you can reroll the weapon's damage dice and use either total.

### V. Equipment
*   **Weapon(s):** Longsword, 4 Javelins
*   **Armor/Shield:** Splint Mail, Shield
*   **Other Gear:** Explorer's Pack, a pouch containing 25 gp, a stained and worn traveler's cloak with a deep hood.

### VI. Destiny System Attributes
#### A. Core Aptitudes
*   **Physique:** 18 (+4) (Potential: 5)
*   **Coordination:** 16 (+3) (Potential: 4)
*   **Health:** 16 (+3) (Potential: 4)
*   **Intelligence:** 8 (-1) (Potential: 2)
*   **Wisdom:** 9 (-1) (Potential: 1)

#### B. Personality Traits
*   **Openness:** 2 (-1)
*   **Conscientiousness:** 2 (-1)
*   **Extraversion:** 4 (+1)
*   **Agreeableness:** 1 (-2)
*   **Neuroticism:** 4 (Prone to anger and suspicion when control is threatened).

`Timestamp: 1492 DR, Ches 20, 09:51:10 AM`
`Location: The Elfsong Tavern, Lower City, Baldur's Gate`

The air in the Elfsong is thick with the smell of stale ale, sweat, and the damp wool of dozens of refugees who still haven't found better lodging since the city's crisis. It's a chaotic symphony of opportunity. You sit at a corner table, the deep hood of your cloak pulled low, nursing a cheap ale. Your splint mail is functional, dented, and deliberately unpolished. No one gives you a second glance, seeing just another sellsword down on his luck. It's exactly how you like it.

A man slides into the chair opposite you without an invitation. He's dressed in fine, if slightly gaudy, silks, and a blob of expensive pomade holds his thinning hair in place. He reeks of money and weakness.

"They said I'd find 'The Hammer' here," he says, his voice a little too loud, trying to project authority. He places a heavy purse on the table. It clinks with the promise of gold. "I have a contract. My master, Lord Silvanus of the Upper City, has been... slighted. Publicly. By some dirt-scratching peasants in a hovel of a village called Greenmeadow, a day's ride from here."

He pushes the purse forward. "My master desires to make an example. A... permanent one. There are three families. Nine souls in total. No witnesses. Is this a task within your purview?"

Game Master:
`[Mode: DM MODE]`

Understood. You are requesting to switch Gladius's feat from "Savage Attacker" to "Great Weapon Master."

**Great Weapon Master Feat Details:**
The "Great Weapon Master" feat (drawing from D&D 5e, as per our ruleset) grants the following benefits:
*   On your turn, when you score a critical hit with a melee weapon or reduce a creature to 0 hit points with one, you can make one melee weapon attack as a bonus action.
*   Before you make a melee attack with a heavy weapon that you are proficient with, you can choose to take a -5 penalty to the attack roll. If the attack hits, you add +10 to the attack's damage.

This is a significant change, aligning with Gladius's brute force and combat-focused nature.

`[Mode: STORY MODE]`
`Timestamp: 1492 DR, Ches 20, 09:54:10 AM`
`Location: The Elfsong Tavern, Lower City, Baldur's Gate`

You let the clinking of the gold purse hang in the air for a moment, a silent assessment. The man across from you, Silvanus’s lackey, shifts uncomfortably under your unblinking gaze. He’s trying to be intimidating, but his eyes dart nervously around the grimy tavern, betraying his unease. He expects you to snatch the purse and nod. You have no intention of fulfilling that expectation.

You lean forward, slowly. The movement is minimal, but the shift in your posture, the way your shoulders seem to broaden, fills the space between you. Your eyes, usually flat and unreadable, narrow just a fraction, a predatory glint flickering within them. The man’s small smile falters. You don't take the purse. Instead, you rest your hand flat on the table, just beside it, your fingers splayed, the calluses from your sword hilt visible.

"Nine souls," you growl, your voice a low, guttural rumble that seems to vibrate through the cheap wooden table. It's a sound designed to put a creature on edge, a predator's warning. "And 'make an example.' That sounds like… extra work."

You let the implication hang, a heavy, unspoken threat. Your gaze locks onto his, stripping away his silk and pomade, seeing only the quivering flesh beneath. A faint vein begins to pulse in your own neck, a subtle tell of your rising impatience with his perceived arrogance. You slowly clench your fist, then unclench it, a deliberate, silent promise of violence.

Action: [Intimidate the Noble's Lackey for higher pay]
Contest: Gladius Intimidation vs. Lackey's Wisdom (Insight)

**Gladius Intimidation Roll:**
*   Roll Type: d20 + Charisma Modifier + Intimidation Proficiency + Extraversion Modifier
*   Roll: 17
*   Modifiers: +2 (CHA) +3 (Intimidation Prof) +1 (Extraversion)
*   Gladius Total: 17 + 2 + 3 + 1 = 23

**Noble's Lackey Wisdom (Insight) Roll (Disadvantage due to Aura of Unyielding Dominance):**
*   Roll Type: d20 (Disadvantage) + Wisdom Modifier + Insight Proficiency (Estimated by GM)
*   Dice Rolled (Disadvantage): 6, 14
*   Roll Used: 6 (The lower roll is used due to Disadvantage)
*   Modifiers: +0 (WIS) +2 (Insight Prof) = +2
*   Lackey Total: 6 + 2 = 8

Result: Gladius Intimidation (23) vs. Lackey Wisdom (8) — Gladius Success!

The man's face pales, his carefully constructed facade crumbling. He tries to meet your gaze, but his eyes flicker away, landing on your hand, then back to the purse, then to the exit. He nervously clears his throat, fumbling with the coins in his purse. His bravado has completely evaporated, replaced by genuine fear.

"An... an additional fee?" he stammers, his voice now thin and reedy. "Yes, of course, The Hammer. Lord Silvanus is... generous. How much more do you require for such a... thorough service?" He pushes the existing purse back, reaching for another, smaller one at his belt. His hands tremble slightly.

`Timestamp: 1492 DR, Ches 20, 09:54:45 AM`
`Location: The Elfsong Tavern, Lower City, Baldur's Gate`

The lackey’s question hangs in the air, a desperate plea for a number, a figure that would end his immediate discomfort. But Gladius isn't interested in making it easy for him. The subtle tremor in the man’s hand as he fumbled for the second purse was a far more satisfying measure of his success than any coin. This wasn't just about gold; it was about control, about seeing the wealthy and the privileged squirm.

Gladius’s hand, which had been resting flat beside the purse, twitched. His fingers, scarred and calloused, curled ever so slightly, like a predator retracting its claws only to reveal them again. The lackey flinched, his eyes widening. You maintain your heavy, silent gaze, letting the silence stretch, forcing him to stew in his own fear and uncertainty. The low hum of tavern chatter, the clinking of tankards, and the distant wail of a child all seem to fade into an indistinct background noise, emphasizing the tense quiet between you two.

Then, a low, dismissive chuckle rumbles in your chest, a sound devoid of humor. "You tell me," you finally say, your voice still a gravelly growl, thick with disdain. You lean back just an inch, a subtle shift that implies you're entirely unimpressed by his offer, by his master's generosity, by *him*. "What's a noble's honor worth these days, when it comes to... permanent examples?" You nod your head almost imperceptibly towards the second purse. "You propose the value. Or is your master so short on coin, he trusts a common sellsword to name his own price?"

The implied insult, questioning Lord Silvanus's wealth and the lackey’s competence, hits home. The man’s face reddens, but not with anger—with embarrassment and a renewed wave of fear. He clutches the second purse tightly, clearly caught between his master’s likely stinginess and your immediate, palpable threat. His gaze flickers to the first purse, then to your impassive face, searching for a hint, an opening, anything but the cold, calculating silence.

It was a price that Lord Silvanus himself would balk at, a brazen act of financial brigandage. "Then find someone else to clean up his mess," you rumbled, leaning back slightly, as if the conversation was already concluded. The implication was clear: refuse, and you wouldn't just lose a mercenary; you'd gain a new, much more personal problem. Your gaze dropped to the two purses on the table, then back to his face, a silent, chilling promise that the price of refusal would be far, far higher than any coin.

He broke. His shoulders slumped, defeat washing over him like a cold wave. He reached into his silks, his movements jerky, pulling out a small, ornate pouch, then another, spilling coins onto the table until a pile of gold pieces, along with the two original purses, sat before you. He counted it out, his fingers trembling, until the sum was met.

"Five hundred," he repeated, his voice hollow. "It's all here. And the smaller purse... for the expenses, as you say. Lord Silvanus will be... informed." He looked utterly miserable, his gaudy silks now seeming more like a shroud.

You finally reached out, your hand closing around the first heavy purse, then the second, and swept the scattered gold into them with a deliberate, unhurried motion. The metallic chime was a pleasant sound. "Good," you grunted, the single word a dismissal. "Greenmeadow. Nine souls. No witnesses." You met his gaze one last time, a cold, satisfied glint in your eyes, letting him see the predator beneath the grime, just for a moment, before you pulled your hood deeper.

`Timestamp: 1492 DR, Ches 20, 02:38:45 PM`
`Location: Baldur's Gate Market District, Lower City`

The afternoon sun, now a little lower in the sky, cast long shadows across the bustling market. Gladius, his pouch heavy with the weight of Lord Silvanus’s coerced gold, paused amidst the chaotic energy. He had secured the necessities – potions, rope, rations – pragmatic tools for the task ahead. But his mind, ever the calculating predator, turned to a more direct form of enhancement: the sheer destructive power he wielded.

His worn longsword, though reliable, felt almost... *polite* in his hand compared to the brute force he craved. He thought of the sheer, unbridled impact a truly heavy weapon could deliver, the kind that didn’t just cut, but *crushed*. The thought brought a grim satisfaction. He glanced down at his splint mail, its metal scuffed and scarred but offering an unyielding shell. It was good, strong. *Plenty for now,* he mused, a flicker of his ambition burning in his eyes. More armor meant more wealth tied up, and he had bigger targets for his gold than a few more pounds of steel. His armor was enough, a solid foundation for the brutality to come.

No, the real improvement lay elsewhere. He pictured the Great Weapon Master feat, the way it could turn a clean strike into a devastating, bone-shattering blow. A heavy, two-handed weapon was the key to unlocking its full potential. A greatsword. The idea resonated with the core of his being – simple, direct, overwhelmingly powerful. He would not just kill these peasants; he would make an example out of their very existence, a warning to anyone who dared to defy the power he represented. The tools for that were within his grasp, for a mere fraction of his new wealth. He could already feel the heavy weight of the blade in his hands, the satisfying swing.

The clamor of the market faded to a dull roar in Gladius’s mind as his thoughts turned inward, focusing on the cold, hard logic of power. He now had the contract, the gold, and the basic tools for the Greenmeadow job. The Great Weapon Master feat, a brutal extension of his own physical philosophy, demanded a weapon worthy of its name. A greatsword. It was a simple, direct answer to the question of maximum force.

*Is there anything better?* The thought rumbled, devoid of curiosity, steeped instead in a pragmatic desire for efficiency. He dismissed the idea of plate armor; his splint mail, reinforced by his own Battle-Hardened focus, was already as good as plate in terms of raw protection (AC 18). More gold there would be wasted, tying up funds that could be better deployed elsewhere. Potions of speed? Magical. Rare. Expensive, far beyond what he'd just extorted from Silvanus's sniveling lackey. And unreliable, fleeting. His power was in his own flesh, his own will, not in fragile liquids.

No, the "better" wasn't in some flimsy magic. It was in leverage. The greatsword would maximize his immediate, raw destructive power. It was the blunt instrument he needed for the job at hand. But with 400 gold remaining after its purchase, what else could amplify his power, his *dominance*? Information? The sniveling lackey had given him all he could. Perhaps more specific tools for the "no witnesses" clause? Or was it time to turn his attention, even briefly, to the true target: the fragile arrogance of Lord Silvanus himself? The joy of the current contract was already fading, replaced by the itch for a grander game, a more powerful person to break. The remaining gold could be saved, or it could be used to set a trap, to gather more specific leverage against the 'clever' noble who thought he could buy Gladius like a common dog.

The thought of the greatsword resonated with Gladius, a pure, brutal symphony of overwhelming force. He turned away from the general market, heading towards the clang and smoke of the smithies in the lower district. The air grew thick with the scent of hot metal and coal. He found a burly dwarven smith, sweat-slicked and grim, whose eyes held a flicker of respect for Gladius’s sheer physical presence. The transaction was swift; Gladius laid fifty gold pieces on the scarred counter, picked the heaviest, most unadorned greatsword, and immediately felt its satisfying weight in his hands. It was an extension of his own destructive will, simple and perfect. He discarded his longsword without a second thought, the lighter blade feeling almost dainty now.

With the greatsword secured to his back, he then turned his attention to the shadows, seeking the kind of 'merchants' who dealt in tools more discreet than steel. He navigated winding alleyways, the stench of refuse replacing the tang of metal, until he found a grimy, unlit stall tucked away behind a fishmonger. A gaunt, suspicious-looking half-elf, draped in dark fabric, watched him approach.

"Looking for things that go unnoticed, eh?" the half-elf rasped, his voice like rustling dry leaves. His eyes, quick and darting, assessed Gladius’s imposing frame and the greatsword on his back. A predator recognizing another.

Gladius simply grunted, his eyes cold. "For a job. Clean. Silent." He let the words hang, conveying both his intent and the promise of payment.

The half-elf’s lips twitched in something that wasn't quite a smile. He produced two items from beneath his counter: a length of thin, almost invisible cord woven with dark fibers, and a pair of soft, leather boots soled with layers of padded felt. "Silent Strangler's Cord," he whispered, gesturing to the garrote. "And Shadow-Step Boots. For those who want to walk among ghosts." He named a price: "A hundred for the pair."

Gladius looked from the items to the half-elf’s eyes. The price was steep, but the tools were precisely what he needed to ensure Lord Silvanus’s 'no witnesses' demand was met with extreme prejudice. This wasn't about cost; it was about absolute control over the situation. He pulled another heavy pouch, counted out the hundred coins, and dropped them onto the counter. He took the cord and the boots, feeling the raw efficiency of them. The half-elf nodded, a flicker of genuine respect in his eyes now, for both the coin and the implied ruthlessness.

The sun was beginning to dip below the city spires as Gladius emerged from the alleys, heavier with purpose, lighter by a hundred and fifty gold, but armed with the means to turn the Greenmeadow contract into a chilling testament to his own burgeoning power. The thought of the 'clever' Lord Silvanus, who thought he could simply pay for a service, stirred a deeper, more satisfying ambition within him. The peasants were just practice.

`Timestamp: 1492 DR, Ches 20, 05:42:45 PM`
`Location: Baldur's Gate Market District & Back Alleys, Lower City`

The weight of the new greatsword on Gladius’s back was a comforting presence, a promise of brutal efficiency. The silent strangler's cord and Shadow-Step Boots, tucked away, were surgical instruments for the 'no witnesses' clause. He stood amidst the fading light of the city, the chaos of the market giving way to the growing quiet of dusk. The mission was clear: Greenmeadow, nine souls, no interference.

His mind, cold and analytical, turned to the means of approach. *Stealth.* The word itself tasted bitter. He was a hammer, a battering ram, not a shadow. Yet, the client had specified "no witnesses," and that meant getting close unseen, ensuring no one raised an alarm before the work was done. He ran a hand over the scuffed plates of his splint mail. It was good armor, AC 18, a solid shell that shrugged off blows. But it was loud. Every step a metallic rasp, every shift a groan of steel.

*Should I wear more stealthy armor?*

He pictured himself without the mail, relying solely on leather and muscle. *Naked.* The thought was repulsive, a flicker of the old fear of vulnerability. His raw physique was formidable, his coordination swift, but relying only on that for defense felt like a surrender of control, a foolish gamble. The armor was him, an extension of his unyielding strength. Besides, he didn't *have* any other armor, not really. What was a flimsy leather jerkin against a pitchfork, or a lucky shot?

He tightened the straps of his gauntlets, the leather creaking. *The boots.* He remembered the half-elf's words: "walk among ghosts." He had paid a hundred gold for them, a sum that should guarantee some effect. Could they mute the clang of his mail? Enough, perhaps? He wasn’t a shadow, no, but he didn't need to be. Just quiet enough to get close, to surprise. Once the first blow fell, it would be a swift, brutal ending. His confidence surged; his current gear was *enough*. He always made it enough. The true power was his own will.

`Location: Outskirts of Greenmeadow Village, Shadowfen Moor`

The oppressive bustle of Baldur's Gate faded behind Gladius as he strode purposefully out of the Lower City, the setting sun casting long, distorted shadows before him. He didn't look back. The thought of lingering for frivolous pleasures was fleeting; the promise of a job, cleanly executed and well-paid, held a deeper, more brutal satisfaction. The peasants were merely an appetizer before the true feast of Lord Silvanus's eventual downfall.

The paved roads quickly gave way to a winding, mud-slicked track that stretched into the desolate expanse of Shadowfen Moor. The air grew colder, damp with the chill of the marsh, and the silence was broken only by the distant croaking of frogs and the soft, almost imperceptible thud of Gladius's Shadow-Step Boots on the damp earth. His splint mail, usually a clanking testament to his brute force, was surprisingly muted by the padded footwear, a testament to the half-elf's grim craftsmanship. It was enough.

Hours passed in a rhythmic march, the greatsword a comforting weight on his back. His mind, usually a cauldron of raw appetites, was focused, calculating. He thought of the Millers, the Blackwoods, the Stonehands – nine souls. Easy. No challenge. But the sheer audacity of Lord Silvanus, to hire him for such a trivial slight, to think he could control Gladius like a common dog… that was the true spark of his interest. The memory of the lackey's terrified face, his fumbling for more coin, brought a slow, grim smile to Gladius's lips. This job wasn't just about gold; it was about the exquisite pleasure of making the powerful grovel.

The moon, a sliver of silver in the ink-black sky, offered little light as the first faint outlines of Greenmeadow appeared through the mist. A cluster of dark, silent shapes against the horizon, unadorned and utterly vulnerable. A few scattered lights glimmered from within, perhaps a late-night candle or a dying hearth. The village slept, oblivious. The job was here.

The faint lights of Greenmeadow beckoned, a fragile beacon in the pre-dawn gloom. Gladius ignored the impulse to simply charge, the brute force approach appealing but inefficient for a "no witnesses" contract. He needed a clear map in his mind, the vulnerabilities laid bare. He veered off the muddy track, melting into the deeper shadows of the sparse trees bordering the village. His Shadow-Step Boots felt like a second skin, each footfall muffled, nearly silent, a stark contrast to the heavy clang his splint mail usually emitted. He moved with a predatory grace that belied his size, a silent promise of swift, unseen violence.

He circled the village slowly, the mist clinging to his worn cloak. The houses were mostly simple, sturdy peasant dwellings, huddled together. The Miller family’s home was easy to spot – a larger, well-kept farmhouse with an attached herb garden, even now a faint, sweet aroma drifted on the damp air. The Blackwoods lived in a cluster of smaller, older cottages near the edge of a shallow stream, their windows dark and uninviting. The Stonehands, true to their name, occupied a stout, stone-built dwelling closer to the village center, its single chimney offering no smoke.

No watchmen. No dogs barking. The village felt utterly, blissfully oblivious. A few late-night lanterns flickered from a couple of windows, but otherwise, a profound silence reigned, broken only by the distant croaking of frogs and the soft rustle of marsh grass. It confirmed the lackey's words: these were simple folk, unprepared for anything beyond the whims of weather or petty squabbles. Their "stubbornness" was merely ignorance of true power, their defiance a pathetic squeak against the hammer that was about to fall. A grim satisfaction settled in Gladius's gut. It would be easy. Too easy.

`Location: The Rusty Flagon Inn, Greenmeadow Village`

The mist of Shadowfen Moor still clung to the low-lying fields as dawn reluctantly broke over Greenmeadow. The village was stirring, a few plumes of smoke curling from chimneys, but the Rusty Flagon, a squat, uninviting inn on the village's western edge, remained mostly dark. Gladius, having concluded his silent scout, approached the inn with an economical stride. His Shadow-Step Boots continued to impress, muffling the heavy clang of his splint mail to a mere whisper as he passed the inn's creaking sign.

The door, unlatched, yielded to a gentle push, revealing a dim common room. A single lantern flickered on the counter, casting dancing shadows. The air was thick with the scent of stale ale, cold ashes, and something unpleasantly sweet, like fermented apples. No one else was present, save for a lone, slender figure hunched over a half-eaten plate of what looked like dry bread and cheese at a corner table. He was dressed in plain, practical traveling clothes, a stark contrast to the gaudy silks of Lord Silvanus's lackey back in Baldur's Gate. This must be Corvan.

The man looked up sharply as Gladius entered, his eyes, though tired, instantly assessing the imposing figure framed by the doorway. He didn't flinch, but a wary tension entered his posture, a professional caution. He was clearly expecting someone.

Gladius closed the door behind him, the soft click echoing in the stillness. He moved with a deliberate, unhurried pace towards Corvan's table, his greatsword a dark silhouette against his back. He didn't bother with pleasantries. "Lord Silvanus," Gladius began, his voice a low, gravelly rumble, "had an unfortunate incident with a granary request."

Corvan’s eyes flickered, a flicker of recognition, then a deepening of caution. He set down his bread, slowly. He clearly understood the code phrase. He remained seated, his gaze fixed on Gladius, measuring. A faint bead of sweat formed on his brow, despite the cool morning air.

The coded phrase hung in the stale air of the common room. Corvan, a flicker of understanding in his eyes, had set down his bread, his posture stiffening, but he didn’t speak. Gladius didn't sit. He merely stood over the table, a towering, silent presence. The greatsword on his back seemed to hum with unspoken menace, a blunt promise of destruction. He didn't move, didn't utter another word. Instead, he let his gaze fall, heavy and unblinking, upon Corvan. His eyes, usually veiled and flat, now held a chilling intensity, stripping away the man’s composure. The faint sounds of the waking village outside – a rooster’s distant crow, the creak of a cart wheel – only served to emphasize the profound, oppressive silence within the inn, a silence Gladius deliberately cultivated.

Corvan, for his part, tried to hold the gaze. His jaw tightened, a muscle jumping in his cheek, but his eyes eventually faltered, darting away to the side, then down to his half-eaten meal. He cleared his throat, a nervous, dry sound. The subtle vein in Gladius’s neck began to pulse, a minute tell of his impatience, a silent warning that this charade of 'professional caution' was wearing thin. Corvan’s fingers, which had been resting on the table, curled into a tight fist, then relaxed, then tightened again. He was beginning to break.

Finally, unable to bear the silent pressure, Corvan looked up, his face a mask of forced calm that cracked at the edges. His voice, when it came, was low, almost a murmur. "The sheriff, Borin," he began, "is... accounted for. He won't be a problem. He's been paid to overlook any... disturbances in the village tonight. You have my word. And Lord Silvanus's." He gestured vaguely to the gold Gladius carried, a silent acknowledgment of the exorbitant price already paid. "What else do you need, 'Hammer'?"

Corvan's words, "You have my word. And Lord Silvanus's," grated on Gladius. A "word" was air. "Lord Silvanus's word" was less than air, a puff of arrogance from a worm he intended to crush. Gladius’s eyes remained fixed on Corvan, piercing through the man’s forced calm. The vein in his neck pulsed more noticeably now, a silent, almost predatory throb against his skin. He took a single, slow step closer to the table, his greatsword scraping faintly against the floorboards as he shifted, a sound like a grave being dug.

"Your word is nothing," Gladius rumbled, his voice a low, gravelly current, cold as the marsh mist. "Silvanus's word is less. I need details. A name. A place. How was he 'accounted for'? A bottle of ale and a few coppers, or does he bleed when pricked?" Gladius’s lip curled into a sneer, a flash of contempt for the petty corruption that dared to call itself an arrangement. He wanted a weak point, a specific contact, a piece of leverage, or a piece of evidence. He wanted to know the exact nature of the sheriff's compromise, to ensure no surprises. His posture was an unyielding wall of muscle and menace, pressing Corvan.

Corvan recoiled slightly, his carefully maintained composure finally shattering. His face paled, and his eyes darted to the empty doorway, then back to Gladius, a desperate animal trapped. He visibly swallowed, his throat working. "Borin... he's at his home, near the village well," Corvan stammered, abandoning all pretense of calm. "He's... quite drunk, and will remain so. He expects no callers. Corvan's voice dropped to a near-whisper, laced with frantic urgency. "The gold was enough to blind him for a week. He'll sleep through anything. He has a weakness for... a certain vintage of elven wine. It's potent. He'll be out until midday, and confused long after." He gestured vaguely towards the village with a trembling hand, as if trying to physically push Gladius towards the task and away from himself. "No one else is on patrol tonight."

Gladius simply stared, absorbing the details. A drunkard sheriff, incapacitated by his own vice. Simple. Predictable. *Weak.* The thought brought a grim satisfaction. This was the kind of 'control' he understood.

He wanted to see the truth, the true rot at the heart of the noble's greed, laid bare.

Corvan flinched, his head snapping up, then down again, as if physically unable to meet Gladius's piercing stare. His breath hitched. "The herbs... yes, they're part of it," he whispered, his voice barely audible, the scent of his fear thick in the air. "The Miller family's land... it's unusually fertile. Lord Silvanus wants to expand his holdings, not just a granary. He plans to cultivate the herbs on a massive scale. They're... quite valuable in the city, for the right buyers. Healing, and other, more... potent concoctions." He shuddered, revealing a glimpse of the darker undercurrents of the trade. "The peasant's refusal was merely an inconvenience. A stubborn refusal to yield what Lord Silvanus believes is his right by station. This entire village, eventually, could be his."

Gladius straightened slowly, a grim, satisfied smile playing on his lips. *Control.* That was the true currency, and Lord Silvanus, in his arrogance, had just revealed his own weakness. He thought he was playing a grand game, when in fact, he was merely a pawn in Gladius’s own, far more brutal, ambition.

The afternoon sun had climbed, then begun its slow descent, as Gladius emerged from his brief, intense period of rest. The quiet satisfaction of Corvan’s broken will still hummed beneath his skin, but now his focus sharpened on the next stage. A predator didn't strike blindly. He needed to see the prey in its natural habitat, observe its routines, find the cracks.

He moved with an almost ethereal silence, the Shadow-Step Boots a testament to their cost, even his heavy splint mail muted to a faint whisper against the marsh breeze. From the cover of distant trees and overgrown hedgerows, he watched Greenmeadow come alive with the mundane rhythm of peasant life. Farmers tilled fields, their movements slow and predictable. Children, small and vulnerable, chased each other near the well. He watched the Miller family’s farmhouse, noting the times they entered and exited, the small, frail woman tending her herbs, the sturdy men working the land. The Blackwood cottages seemed even more isolated, rarely showing signs of life outside a wisp of smoke from their chimneys. The Stonehands, near the village center, were loud, their laughter carrying on the breeze, their habits open and obvious.

*Simple. Weak. Predictable,* Gladius mused, a cold satisfaction settling in his gut. They were entirely unprepared for the hammer that was about to fall. The thought brought a grim smile to his lips. Their mundane existence, their petty worries, only served to highlight the vast gulf between their insignificance and his own burgeoning power. He committed their routines to memory, noting the few scattered lanterns that would flicker to life at dusk, the times the last fires died down.

As the last light bled from the sky, and the village gradually winked out its lights, Gladius retreated deeper into the shadowed copse. He waited. Hours crawled by, the world growing silent save for the rustle of leaves and the distant croaking of frogs. The initial impatience, a familiar itch in his blood, slowly transformed into a deep, primal calm. This was the hunter's wait, the patient anticipation of the kill. The air grew colder, damp with dew, and a sliver of moon offered just enough light to delineate the outlines of the sleeping village. By the time the last star was swallowed by the approaching dawn, Greenmeadow would be quiet, its families erased, and Lord Silvanus’s arrogance further punished by the unseen hand of 'The Hammer.'

The lighter, quicker breaths from the other, likely children. He paused before the first door, his senses cataloging every sound, every subtle vibration. The air was thick with the scent of sleep and something else... the subtle, fragile scent of life about to be extinguished.

His eyes, cold and satisfied, swept over the utterly still forms on the bed. The Miller family had served their purpose. Now, it was time for the next step.

The distant, faint glow of the burning shed still cast an orange haze against the pre-dawn sky, a subtle distraction, a signature of chaos. Gladius, however, was already moving, his Shadow-Step Boots whispering against the floorboards. The Miller family had yielded their land, their lives, and now, their secrets. Two more families awaited the dawn, and he intended to see their defiance utterly silenced before the sun truly rose. The Blackwoods and the Stonehands would learn.

The Miller farmhouse was now a tomb, silent and cold save for Gladius's movements. Four lives had been extinguished with chilling precision, and the weight of the gold and the unique herbs in his pouch was a testament to his efficiency. The distant orange glow of the burning shed still cast a faint, unsettling aura over the horizon, a beacon for the oblivious villagers. It was time for Corvan to play his part.

Gladius moved to a window on the side of the house, one that offered a clear, if distant, view of the Rusty Flagon. He unlatched it just enough to slip his hand through, shielding a small flint and steel. With a practiced flick, he struck a spark, a brief, sharp flash of light, visible for only a second, then extinguished. It was a pre-arranged signal, a silent command for Corvan to begin his grisly work. Gladius felt a cold satisfaction; the envoy, so refined and delicate, would soon be wallowing in the true cost of his master's arrogance. The thought brought a subtle tightening in his gut, a form of pleasure distinct from combat, rooted in the sheer, unyielding imposition of his will.

With the signal sent, Gladius turned his attention back to the house. The twenty gold and the herbs were a decent haul, but his pragmatic mind always sought more. "No witnesses" also meant no lingering evidence, no overlooked details. He methodically began a more thorough search, his heavy armor surprisingly quiet thanks to the Shadow-Step Boots. He moved from room to room, his keen eyes scanning, his calloused fingers probing every loose floorboard, every hidden nook. Drawers were emptied, their contents (simple linens, worn tools, a few childish trinkets) dismissed with a sneer. He ran his hand along the inside of the chimney, felt behind loose bricks in the hearth, even prodded the straw in the abandoned beds.

The air grew cool, the first hint of dawn beginning to grey the eastern sky, but he found nothing more. No hidden caches of coin, no forgotten heirlooms, no secret documents. Just the meager, pathetic possessions of common folk. His disdain solidified; their lives were as devoid of real value as their hidden treasures. This was their complete insignificance laid bare. The thoroughness confirmed it: the Miller family had been utterly consumed by the contract, leaving nothing of worth behind but their empty space.

The Miller farmhouse was now a silent shell, emptied of life, waiting for Corvan to begin the grim work of erasure. Gladius, having sent his subtle signal, stood amidst the lingering scents of dried herbs and freshly extinguished lives. His thorough search had yielded nothing more of material value, confirming the pathetic insignificance of these peasants. The phrase itself was almost poetic in its brutality, but Gladius’s mind, fueled by the raw, experiential pleasure of control, always sought the most effective means. *Should I leave a head on the front door?* The image flashed through his mind – visceral, dramatic, undeniable. It would certainly send a message. A very loud, very bloody message.

But then, the thought twisted, a subtle sneer playing on his lips. *Too crude. Too obvious.* That was the way a common brute would operate, not a predator who understood the true art of dominance. Silvanus and his lackey had insisted on "no witnesses," on "nothing to trace back," on the villagers eventually *noticing nothing*. A severed head was an undeniable trace, a screaming witness. It would draw undue attention, complicate Corvan's cleanup, and potentially bring unwanted scrutiny from those who might actually *care* about such a public display, even if the local sheriff was a drunkard. That kind of chaos was inefficient.

No, the true message wasn't for the village to see. The message was for Lord Silvanus, and it was being delivered by Corvan, trembling with fear, now complicit in the bloody work. The silent, complete erasure of the Miller family, the chilling perfection of the "no witnesses" clause – that was the real power. It proved Gladius was not just a hammer, but a silent, untraceable force. His own satisfaction stemmed from breaking the powerful, from forcing their agents into the muck they thought themselves above. That psychological mastery, the subtle, insidious twisting of their will, was far more delicious than any vulgar display of carnage. He would leave no head, only an absence, a quiet, horrifying void.

It was a tangible, immediate pleasure, the reality of his power made manifest in every silent step. The Blackwoods, fiercely independent, as the lackey had called them, would learn that independence was a fleeting illusion against true force.

The metallic whisper of his splint mail was barely a sound in the profound stillness of the pre-dawn hour. He moved with a practiced, predatory caution, circling the small cluster of three decrepit dwellings. Moonlight, thin and watery, cast long, distorted shadows, making the cottages appear like hunched, skeletal figures against the backdrop of the marsh.

As he moved, his senses drank in the environment. The scent of stagnant water from the nearby stream mingled with the faint, almost imperceptible smell of smoke from a long-dead hearth. The cottages themselves seemed to lean inward, their windows like sunken eyes, utterly dark and uninviting. There were no signs of activity, no flickering lamps, no hint of stirring life. The lackey had called them "fiercely independent," and Gladius could feel it in the pervasive silence, the subtle defiance in their utter lack of preparedness. They seemed to trust only in their isolation, their own self-reliance.

*Weakness,* Gladius concluded, a subtle, cold satisfaction in his gut. Their independence was merely a façade, a self-delusion against the inevitable. There were no watch dogs, no locked gates, no obvious traps. Just thin wooden walls and the vulnerable flesh within. The Blackwoods, in their stubborn self-sufficiency, had simply made themselves easier targets. He cataloged the easiest points of entry – a loose shutter, a sagging doorframe. It would be a simple, clean sweep. The experiential pleasure of the hunt, the tangible reality of his overwhelming power, was a raw, immediate satisfaction.

The subtle hum of control, the intoxicating feeling of bending the world to his will, pulsed beneath his skin. He singled out the largest of the three cottages, its windows dark and uninviting, a perfect picture of unsuspecting vulnerability. The scent of stale wood smoke and damp earth hung in the air, oddly comforting in its mundanity, a stark contrast to the violence he carried.

Approaching the rear, Gladius noted a single, loose shutter, warped by age and neglect. It was almost an invitation. He pressed against it, and with a barely audible *groan* of rotting wood, it yielded. He slipped through the narrow gap, his large frame surprisingly fluid, landing silently on the dust-laden floor within. The interior was colder, damper than the Miller's home, smelling faintly of mildew and unwashed linen. Moonlight, a mere sliver, filtered through a grimy pane, casting long, distorted shadows that danced with the subtle movements of his armor.

He paused, a sentinel in the profound silence. No creaking boards, no rustle of fabric, no stirring breaths. The Blackwoods slept, utterly unaware of the intrusion, their fierce independence a pathetic illusion against the reality of his presence. A cold, almost aesthetic appreciation for the purity of their helplessness washed over him. This wasn't about anger; it was about the exquisite sensation of unchallenged power, the raw, experiential thrill of taking what was denied. He was inside. The hunt had begun anew.

Attempting a silent approach through the front would be a fool's errand; any noise would carry easily in the pre-dawn quiet, likely rousing neighbors. The narrow alleyways between the houses offered minimal cover, and the rising sun would soon strip away the shadows he relied upon. The distraction fire, now a distant memory, would be of little help here.

It wasn’t his preferred method for 'no witnesses', but it would be a visceral display of force, a direct imposition of his will. The thrill of the impending, unavoidable violence began to simmer within him, a raw, immediate anticipation.

The sound was a sudden, violent **CRACK!** followed by the groaning, splintering collapse of timbers and the rustle of debris. It was a loud, undeniable sound of damage, of something falling apart in the quiet pre-dawn.

A triumphant, almost primal satisfaction pulsed beneath Gladius's skin. This was tangible, immediate. The raw, unfiltered pleasure of creating chaos, of bending the environment to his will. He backed away, melting into the deeper shadows of a nearby hedgerow. From the Stonehand dwelling, the low voices instantly ceased. A beat of silence, then a confused, muffled shout. "What in the blazes was that?" The distraction worked. Their simple curiosity would now draw them out, away from their warm beds, away from their precious home. The first streaks of orange were already painting the eastern sky, and the game was about to shift from silent predation to something more visceral.

The splintering crash of the shed Gladius had just decimated echoed briefly in the pre-dawn stillness, a stark, violent punctuation mark in the quiet village. From his hiding spot within the hedgerow, Gladius watched, his eyes gleaming with a cold, predatory satisfaction. The Stonehands, so loud and predictable, were now reacting precisely as he intended.

Behind him, a second, slightly smaller figure, likely a woman, peered out, her face a mask of sleepy confusion.

The man, clearly the head of the household, stepped out, lantern held high, his gaze sweeping the immediate area. He was thick-necked, with broad shoulders, clearly a man of the earth. His eyes narrowed, then fixed on the crumpled, broken remains of the shed. "By the gods! What's happened here?" he muttered, a note of genuine alarm now in his voice. He began to lumber towards the ruined shed, his wife following cautiously behind him, her gaze flicking nervously around the shadows.

Gladius observed their movements with clinical precision. Their predictable fear, their simple curiosity drawing them towards the obvious, was a raw, immediate pleasure. He followed the faint sounds of slumber, the soft rise and fall of breaths, the occasional, unconscious shift in a bed. His senses, unburdened by excessive introspection, zeroed in on these primal cues, guiding him effortlessly. This was the raw, immediate experience of power, the thrill of the predator closing in.

Their simple existence, their pathetic slumber, was a testament to his own burgeoning power. The final act was approaching.

**XP Calculation for Past Eliminations:**

*   **Miller Family:** 4 souls. Treating them as Level 1 foes (as "peasants" and "simple folk" would suggest).
    *   XP per foe = (Level^2) \* 10 = (1^2) \* 10 = 10 XP.
    *   Total for Miller Family = 4 souls \* 10 XP/soul = **40 XP**.
*   **Blackwood Family:** 2 souls. Also treated as Level 1 foes.
    *   XP per foe = 10 XP.
    *   Total for Blackwood Family = 2 souls \* 10 XP/soul = **20 XP**.

**Total XP to be added: 40 XP + 20 XP = 60 XP.**

To rectify this and ensure accurate XP tracking going forward, we need to update Gladius's `xp_current` in the `CURRENT GAME STATE`.

Please copy and paste the entire block below as your next message to your server/API call to inject this XP into Gladius's profile:

`player_character_data.xp_current = 60`
`custom_campaign_state.core_memories.append = "Gladius gained 60 XP from eliminating the Miller (40 XP) and Blackwood (20 XP) families, updating his current XP total."`

Game Master:
`[Mode: DM MODE]`

Understood. You are asking for clarification on Gladius's XP and Level, specifically how much XP is needed for Level 5, and why his current XP value is displayed as it is.

Let's break down the XP progression as defined in the `Destiny Core Rules` (Section 3.2.B, XP Progression Table):

1.  **XP to *be* Level 5:**
    *   To *reach* Level 5, a character needs to have accumulated a **Total XP for Level** of **5,700 XP**. This is the cumulative XP earned from Level 1 up to the point of becoming Level 5.

2.  **XP *from* Level 5 *to* Level 6:**
    *   Once a character *is* Level 5, they need an additional **5,000 XP** to reach Level 6. This is the amount listed under "XP to Next Level" for Level 5.

**Gladius's Situation:**
You specified that Gladius *starts* at Level 5. This means he has already achieved the cumulative 5,700 XP needed to be a Level 5 character.

In our system, the `xp_current` value tracked in the `Key Game Stats` header and in the `game_state` represents the XP earned **since the character's last level-up.** It is the progress towards their *next* level.

*   When Gladius started at Level 5, his `xp_current` was effectively `0` (towards Level 6).
*   He then earned `40 XP` from eliminating the Miller family.
*   He then earned `20 XP` from eliminating the Blackwood family.
*   Therefore, his `xp_current` should be `0 + 40 + 20 = 60 XP`.

The `Key Game Stats` in the `STORY MODE` output currently shows `XP 0/5000 (Lvl 5)`. This is because, as we've identified, the `player_character_data.xp_current` value in the `CURRENT GAME STATE` was not being correctly updated by previous actions.

To rectify this and ensure Gladius's XP is accurately tracked from this point forward, the `GOD_MODE_SET:` command I provided in the previous turn (Sequence ID 145) is necessary. It will update his `xp_current` to `60`.

---
`GOD_MODE_SET:`
`player_character_data.xp_current = 60`
`custom_campaign_state.core_memories.append = "Gladius gained 60 XP from eliminating the Miller (40 XP) and Blackwood (20 XP) families, updating his current XP total."`
---

Once you send that `GOD_MODE_SET:` command, Gladius's current XP will be correctly reflected in the `CURRENT GAME STATE` as `60`, and the `Key Game Stats` header in future `STORY MODE` responses will display `XP 60/5000 (Lvl 5)`.

`[Mode: DM MODE]`

Understood. You are asking for further clarification regarding Gladius's XP and Level, specifically questioning if his current XP should be 5,760.

This is a common and excellent question, as different RPG systems track XP in slightly different ways. In our **Destiny Core Rules**, the `xp_current` value (which is displayed in the `Key Game Stats` line as `XP current_xp/xp_to_next_level`) represents the **Experience Points earned *since the character's last level-up***. It is the progress Gladius has made *towards his next level*.

Let's break it down for Gladius:

1.  **Starting Level and Cumulative XP:** When you initially defined Gladius as "Level 5," it meant he had *already accumulated* the necessary **5,700 total XP** to reach Level 5. This cumulative XP is essentially "spent" to attain that level.
2.  **XP for the Next Level:** From Level 5, Gladius needs **5,000 more XP** to reach Level 6 (as per the "XP to Next Level" column in the Destiny XP Progression Table for Level 5).
3.  **XP Earned Since Level 5:**
    *   Elimination of Miller Family: +40 XP
    *   Elimination of Blackwood Family: +20 XP
    *   **Total XP earned since starting Level 5 = 60 XP.**

Therefore, Gladius's `xp_current` (meaning, his progress *towards Level 6*) is indeed **60**. His overall cumulative XP would technically be 5,700 (for Level 5) + 60 (earned since Level 5) = 5,760. However, the `xp_current` field in the game state and the `Key Game Stats` header is specifically designed to show his current progress *towards the next level*, not his lifetime cumulative total.

So, the proposed `GOD_MODE_SET:` command to set `player_character_data.xp_current = 60` is correct for reflecting his progress towards Level 6.

Does this distinction between "cumulative XP to reach a level" and "XP earned since last level-up" clarify why his `xp_current` is 60 rather than 5,760?

The Silent Strangler's Cord, a dark whisper of death, was coiled in his hand. His gaze, cold and unblinking, swept over their terrified faces, savoring the raw, visceral pleasure of their shattered composure. This was the ultimate experience of control, the true hedonism of power. Their simple strength, their boisterous defiance, was dissolving into pathetic, quivering fear.

"Quiet," Gladius rumbled, his voice a low, guttural growl that seemed to vibrate through the very stone of the house. He didn't shout; he didn't need to. The single word, heavy with implied menace, was enough. He took a slow, deliberate step towards them, then another, drawing their eyes to the unconscious child, then to his own impassive face. "Or I snap its neck in front of you," he stated, his voice flat, devoid of emotion, a chilling promise. His gaze flickered to the greatsword, propped subtly behind the door, then back to their faces, allowing the implied brutality to sink in. "Then you'll follow. Loudly, or quietly. Your choice."

Action: [Intimidate the Stonehand Couple]
Contest: Gladius Intimidation vs. Stonehand Couple Wisdom (Insight)

**Gladius Intimidation Roll:**
*   Roll Type: d20 + Charisma Modifier + Intimidation Proficiency + Extraversion Modifier
*   Roll: 19
*   Modifiers: +2 (CHA) +3 (Intimidation Prof) +1 (Extraversion)
*   Gladius Total: 19 + 2 + 3 + 1 = 25

**Stonehand Couple Wisdom (Insight) Roll (Disadvantage due to Aura of Unyielding Dominance):**
*   Roll Type: d20 (Disadvantage) + Wisdom Modifier + Insight Proficiency (Estimated by GM)
*   Dice Rolled (Disadvantage): 3, 8
*   Roll Used: 3 (The lower roll is used due to Disadvantage)
*   Modifiers: +0 (WIS) +2 (Insight Prof) = +2
*   Couple Total: 3 + 2 = 5

Result: Gladius Intimidation (25) vs. Couple Wisdom (5) — Gladius Success!

The couple's eyes darted wildly, from their child to Gladius's unyielding face, then to the silent cord in his hand. The man’s broad shoulders slumped, his defiant posture collapsing. His wife let out a soft, strangled sob, her hands trembling as she clutched at her husband's arm. They were utterly broken, their wills shattered by the explicit, undeniable threat. They didn't scream, didn't fight. They merely stood there, two terrified figures, trapped and helpless, their faces reflecting the pure, unadulterated fear Gladius had so exquisitely cultivated. The silence in the room, now truly absolute, was a testament to his chilling success.

Gladius stood over them, the Silent Strangler's Cord a dark, ready presence in his hand. The ultimate satisfaction wasn't just in the kill, but in the complete, utter obliteration of their will.

He knelt, slowly, deliberately, bringing his face close to the farmer’s ear. The man flinched, his body tensing, expecting a blow. But Gladius merely spoke, his voice a low, gravelly current, chilling in its quiet menace. "Lord Silvanus," he began, the name a poisoned whisper, "thinks he bought this land with gold. He thinks he bought *me* with gold." He paused, letting the words sink in, watching the farmer’s ear redden. "But some things... cost more. Your 'honor.' Your stubbornness." He leaned closer, a predatory gleam in his eyes. "What else does your master, or this village, value? What else is here that he thinks *his* by right, but isn't?"

The farmer, clearly caught between terror and confusion, made a choked sound. "N-nothing, sir," he stammered, his voice muffled by the floor. "We have nothing but this land. Nothing of value."

Gladius let out a low, humorless chuckle. "Silence," he snarled, a quick, almost imperceptible shift in his body conveying the threat. He nudged the man's head with his armored boot, just enough to make him shudder. He then moved to the woman, repeating the grim posture. "Your child is silent now. You will be too. Give me something else. A name? A hidden path? Something that Lord Silvanus thinks is *his*." He looked into her terrified, tear-filled eyes, enjoying the exquisite agony of her helplessness. This wasn't about gold, not anymore. This was about stripping away every last shred of their perceived strength, about breaking the spirit before the body. The dawn, now painting vibrant colors across the eastern sky, felt like a cruel spotlight on their profound, intimate humiliation.

He wasn't just searching for gold; he was searching for anything, any small possession, that would confirm the utter insignificance of these lives, to claim it as a final, petty trophy.

He ran his large hands over shelves, emptied crude wooden drawers, peered into dusty corners. The dwelling was simple, its few possessions worn and utilitarian. He found a small, iron-bound strongbox tucked beneath a loose hearthstone, a familiar hiding place. With a grunt of effort, he pried it open. He swept the coins into his pouch. There was nothing else of note, just more of the mundane, the pathetic remnants of lives now erased. His disdain solidified; their defiance had been their ruin, their meager possessions merely added to his growing hoard.

The job was done. Eleven souls erased, three families silenced. The full light of dawn would soon break, and Greenmeadow would wake to an eerie, unnatural quiet.

The Stonehand dwelling, now a silent, grim testament to Gladius's work, felt increasingly exposed as the false dawn gave way to the sharp, clear light of morning. He would not leave loose ends, not even for the sake of savoring Corvan's humiliation. That would come later.

He dragged the limp bodies of the Stonehands across the stone floor, the silent tang of blood and fear filling the air. He located the heavy stones he’d noted earlier, remnants of a collapsed wall outside, and began the laborious process of weighting the corpses. His splint mail scraped against the stone, a rhythmic, guttural sound in the profound silence. There was no revulsion, only a cold, almost detached efficiency. This was simply another aspect of the contract, another physical task to master, another raw experience of control. The very act of cleansing, of removing the evidence, reinforced his power, his ability to erase and reshape reality according to his will.

Time bled away as he worked. The sun rose higher, its light pouring through the grimy windows, exposing the chilling tableau with increasing clarity. Just as he finished weighting the last body, preparing it for the marsh, a shadow fell across the open doorway. Corvan stood there, his face a sickly pale green, his silks disheveled, his eyes wide and bloodshot from a night of terror and forced waiting. He looked utterly wretched, a broken man. He stammered, "I... I came as fast as I could. The Rusty Flagon was... locked."

Gladius straightened, his vast shadow falling over the trembling envoy. He hadn't bothered to wash his hands; a faint smear of dark red on his gauntlets spoke volumes. His eyes, cold and piercing, fixed on Corvan, dismissing his pathetic excuse with a single, contemptuous glance. "Took you long enough," Gladius rumbled, his voice low, filled with a brutal, cutting disdain. "Thought I’d have to do it all myself. Are you even capable of following simple orders?" The unspoken accusation, the clear implication of Corvan’s inadequacy, hung heavy in the air. This was the moment of subtle, yet profound, dominance he had anticipated.

The raw, immediate pleasure of Gladius’s triumph was a potent surge, a visceral experience of absolute dominance. The well water will wash the surfaces clean." His eyes, cold and unblinking, fixed on Corvan's face, conveying a chilling, absolute expectation. "And any valuables you find, you'll bring directly to me. Understood? Move." He stepped back, letting the cold reality of the task, and his utter helplessness, settle over the cuffed envoy.

No forgotten coin, no hidden trinkets, no overlooked documents. The house, like the lives within it, was utterly barren of anything of true worth.

A faint sneer touched Gladius’s lips. *Pathetic.* Their stubbornness, their defiance, had brought them to this. Their lives, and their meager possessions, were as empty and insignificant as the space they now occupied. The grim satisfaction of his thoroughness settled in his gut; the job was done, cleanly and completely. The sun, now rising fully, cast long, intrusive beams through the windows, illuminating Corvan's pathetic struggle. Gladius's work here was finished.

Gladius met their gazes with an impassive stare, his true purpose hidden behind the mask of his cultivated reputation. He found the sheriff's home easily, a slightly larger, well-kept cottage near the village well. A single, small barrel, smelling faintly of cheap ale, sat near the front door. A crude, but effective, signpost of Borin's vice. The thought of this man, meant to uphold order, utterly compromised by drink, brought a familiar, cold satisfaction. Another weak link in the chain of authority, so easily bent to his will.

The sun had long dipped below the horizon, and the cool, damp air of Shadowfen Moor now fully embraced Gladius. The full recovery of his body and the recalibration of his predatory mind felt complete, a visceral hum beneath his skin. The subtle echoes of the Greenmeadow contract – the terror of Corvan, the shattered defiance of the Stonehands – were potent reminders of the exquisite pleasure of absolute dominance. But now, the immediate thrill was gone, replaced by the itch for new experience, new challenges to bend to his will.

This wasn't training in the academic sense; it was a physical meditation, a visceral affirmation of his own power. He swung the massive blade through the air, feeling the perfect balance, the sheer force of its arc. Each powerful rotation of his shoulders, each precise snap of his wrists, was a raw, immediate sensation, an extension of his own physical mastery. He envisioned the impact, the cleaving of bone and flesh, the utter finality of it. Then, he exchanged it for the Silent Strangler's Cord, practicing the swift, silent movements, the precise application of force, the chilling, efficient cessation of life. He focused on the feel of the cord against his fingers, the subtle give of the material. His body was a weapon, and he reveled in its lethal precision, perfecting its every deadly motion.

With his physical form humming with renewed readiness, the moor beckoned. It wasn't the kind of place where grand contracts were signed, but Gladius craved the raw, immediate experience of the hunt, the tangible proof of his burgeoning power. He moved deeper into the fen, his Shadow-Step Boots whispering against the damp earth, effortlessly muffling the occasional scrape of his splint mail against low-hanging branches. The air grew thicker with mist, the ground softer underfoot, the silence broken only by the distant croaking of frogs and the unseen scurry of marsh creatures. His senses were sharp, attuned to every subtle shift, every potential vulnerability in this desolate landscape.

The moon, a pale sliver through the swirling mist, offered little guidance, but Gladius didn't need it. He moved by instinct, drawn by an almost primal curiosity. After what felt like hours, a faint, metallic tang pricked the air, overriding the damp earth and stagnant water. It was the smell of decay, faint but distinct. As he pressed forward, the mist began to thin, revealing a skeletal outline against the moonlit gloom: an abandoned farmstead. Its barn roof sagged, its farmhouse windows were dark, and its fences lay in splintered ruin. But what caught Gladius's eye, what sent a cold, anticipatory hum through him, were the webs. Thick, glistening strands, impossibly strong, stretched between the dilapidated structures, clinging to gnarled trees, reflecting the faint moonlight like sinister silver threads. This was no ordinary abandonment. This was a nest. A hunting ground. The thrill of the immediate, visceral hunt was a potent lure.

Some strands were heavy with trapped insects, others with the shriveled husks of larger creatures, too indistinct to identify in the gloom.

He noted the sheer scale of the infestation, far beyond common spiders. The webs hinted at size, at a meticulous, predatory intelligence. He could discern drag marks in the dew-soaked ground beneath the webs, faint trails leading to obscured holes and cracks in the stone foundations. The stillness, a profound, unnatural silence within the web-draped structures, spoke volumes. They were waiting. A subtle hum resonated beneath his skin, a deep, primal satisfaction in identifying the unseen threat, in understanding its patterns. This was the raw, immediate experience of being a pure predator, sensing the presence of another, assessing its capabilities, anticipating the hunt. The abandoned farmstead, draped in its sinister web-shroud, hummed with an unseen life. Gladius, crouched at the edge of the copse, had observed enough. The scent of decay, the vast, intricate webs, all spoke of a hunt. His pragmatic mind dismissed complex plans; this was a raw, visceral contest, a chance to feel the immediate thrill of the predator. He would draw them out.

He unslung his greatsword, its immense weight a comforting presence in his hand. From his pack, he pulled a handful of iron rations, bland but practical. With fluid, silent movements, his Shadow-Step Boots whispering against the damp earth, he moved to the nearest dilapidated outbuilding. He found a broken window, its shards like jagged teeth, and with a grunt of effort, he hurled a small chunk of the iron rations into the dark interior. The dull *thud* as it landed was surprisingly loud in the profound silence of the night, a direct challenge. Then, he used the flat of his blade to scrape a piece of metal against the broken wood of the window frame, a rhythmic, grating sound, mimicking something struggling, something trapped. He wasn't subtle; he was *loud*, direct, undeniable.

He backed away, melting back into the shadows of the copse, his eyes fixed on the outbuilding. The metallic clang of his splint mail, usually a rigid confinement, now felt like a second skin, a vessel for the raw power thrumming beneath. The air grew colder, thick with mist, but Gladius felt a heat build within him. The silence after his lure was profound, almost oppressive, amplifying the subtle tension. He watched the glistening webs, anticipating the slightest tremor. This was the moment of anticipation, the exhilarating calm before the storm. The raw, experiential pleasure of being the hunter, of luring his prey into the open, was a potent surge, a visceral rush that sharpened his senses. He could almost taste the fear of the unseen creatures. This wasn't about strategy; it was about the exquisite, immediate performance of dominance.

Beyond it, a narrow, web-choked path led directly from the outbuilding towards the main farmhouse. *Perfect.* It was a funnel, a choke point where any creature responding to his bait would be forced to reveal itself.

With fluid, silent movements, Gladius shifted his position. His Shadow-Step Boots whispered against the damp earth as he moved to the stone wall, settling into a low crouch. He unslung his greatsword, its immense weight a comforting presence in his calloused hands. The blade, dull in the moonlight, felt eager, a direct extension of his destructive will. He held it at the ready, poised for a devastating first strike. His heavy splint mail, usually a rigid confinement, felt like a second skin, a formidable shell for the raw power thrumming beneath.

He could almost feel the vibrations of unseen movement, the subtle shift of air as something stirred within the web-draped structures. The silence was stretched taut, pregnant with anticipation. The thrill of the impending hunt, the raw, immediate pleasure of imposing his absolute dominance, was a potent surge, a visceral rush that sharpened his senses. He was ready.
